\displaystyle:\delta_{\eta^{*}}\leq\frac{\epsilon(1-\gamma)^{2}}{2(\gamma+2)}.

When
\eta^{*}=(1-\gamma)/\tau
, the iteration complexity is now proportional to the effective horizon
1/(1-\gamma)
modulo some log factor,
where the
iteration complexity and
\delta_{\text{max}}

are
now independent of the choice of the regularization parameter
\tau
.

Lemma 3

(
Sample complexity to guarantee the optimal threshold
4\epsilon
of
2

)
.

We define

\delta_{\text{max}}
as
right-hand side
of the equation (
D.36
)
. If
we have the number of samples per state-action pairs
is at
least the order of

\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{3}\delta^{2}_{\text{max}}}

up to some logarithmic factor,
then

\delta\leq\delta_{\text{max}}
holds with high probability and we can guarantee the optimal threshold
4\epsilon
with high probability for the upper bound of
2
, provided
(
D.37
), (
D.38
) and (
D.39
) hold.

Proof of Theorem
2
.

1. ProST-T
\iota^{(k)}_{H}
:

The
empirical
estimated model prediction error
\iota^{(k+1)}_{h}(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})
is represented as follows (Definition (
C.11
))
:

\displaystyle-\iota^{(k+1)}_{h}(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})=

\displaystyle-R_{(k+1)}(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})-\gamma(P_{(k+1)}\widehat{V}^{\widehat{\pi}^{(k+1)},(k+1)}_{h+1})(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})

\displaystyle+\widehat{Q}^{\widehat{\pi}^{(k+1)},(k+1)}_{h}(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})

(D.40)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-R_{(k+1)}(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})-\gamma(P_{(k+1)}\widehat{V}^{\widehat{\pi}^{(k+1)},(k+1)}_{h+1})(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})

\displaystyle+\widehat{R}_{(k+1)}(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})+\gamma(\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}\widehat{V}^{\widehat{\pi}^{(k+1)},(k+1)}_{h+1})(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})

(D.41)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{R}_{(k+1)}-R_{(k+1)}\right)(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})

\displaystyle+\gamma\left(\left(\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}-P_{(k+1)}\right)\widehat{V}^{\widehat{\pi}^{(k+1)},(k+1)}_{h+1}\right)(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bar{\Delta}^{Bonus,r}_{(k),h}+\gamma\left|\left|\left(\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}-P_{(k+1)}\right)(\cdot~|~s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})\right|\right|_{1}\left|\left|\widehat{V}^{\widehat{\pi}^{(k+1)},(k+1)}_{h+1}(\cdot)\right|\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bar{\Delta}^{Bonus,r}_{(k),h}+\gamma\bar{\Delta}^{p}_{k,h}\frac{\gamma^{H-h}\hat{r}_{\text{max}}}{1-\gamma}

(D.42)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bar{\Delta}^{r}_{(k),h}+2\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})+\gamma\bar{\Delta}^{p}_{(k),h}\frac{\gamma^{H-h}\hat{r}_{\text{max}}}{1-\gamma}

(D.43)

) holds due to the future

), the equation (

) holds since

, and the equation (

for all

.

(D.48)

2. ProST-T

(D.58)

Finally, the

can be bounded

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{I}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\bar{\iota}_{\infty}^{K}-\iota^{K}_{H}+C_{p}\sqrt{K-1}

\displaystyle+(K-1)\sqrt{H}\left(\lambda r_{\text{max}}+2\beta+\frac{\gamma\hat{r}_{\text{max}}}{1-\gamma}\left(|\mathcal{S}|\sqrt{\frac{H^{2}}{2}\log{\left(\frac{H}{\delta\lambda}\right)}}+\lambda\right)\right)\sqrt{\frac{1}{w}}\sqrt{\log{\left(\frac{\lambda+wH}{\lambda}\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\left(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+H\right)B_{r}(\Delta_{\pi})+\frac{(1+H\hat{r}_{\text{max}})\gamma}{1-\gamma}B_{p}(\Delta_{\pi})\right)w

\displaystyle+(K-1)\sqrt{H}\left(\lambda r_{\text{max}}+2\beta+\frac{\gamma\hat{r}_{\text{max}}}{1-\gamma}\left(|\mathcal{S}|\sqrt{\frac{H^{2}}{2}\log{\left(\frac{H}{\delta\lambda}\right)}}+\lambda\right)\right)\sqrt{\frac{1}{w}}\sqrt{\log{\left(\frac{\lambda+wH}{\lambda}\right)}}

\displaystyle+C_{p}\sqrt{K-1}

Now,
let

be a

and

as

\displaystyle B(\Delta_{\pi})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+H\right)B_{r}(\Delta_{\pi})+\frac{(1+H\hat{r}_{\text{max}})\gamma}{1-\gamma}B_{p}(\Delta_{\pi})

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq}\left(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+H\right)\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{r}}B_{r}(1)+\frac{(1+H\hat{r}_{\text{max}})\gamma}{1-\gamma}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{p}}B_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=C_{B_{r}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{r}}+C_{B_{p}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{p}}

(D.60)

where

and

C_{B_{p}}=\frac{(1+H\hat{r}_{\text{max}})\gamma}{1-\gamma}B_{p}(1)

are
constants related
to the
total variation budget with reward and transition probability
.

Recall the
definitions
of
B_{r}(\Delta_{\pi})

and

, as well as the inequalities

B_{r}(\Delta_{\pi})\leq\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{r}}B_{r}(1)

B_{p}(\Delta_{\pi}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~\leq~}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{p}}B_{p}(1)
. We denote

and

as time-elapsing variation
budgets
for one policy iteration. We also let the constant

be defined
as

\displaystyle C_{k}

\displaystyle=(K-1)\sqrt{H}\left(\lambda r_{\text{max}}+2\beta+\frac{\gamma\hat{r}_{\text{max}}}{1-\gamma}\left(|\mathcal{S}|\sqrt{\frac{H^{2}}{2}\log{\left(\frac{H}{\delta\lambda}\right)}}+\lambda\right)\right).

Then,
an
upper bound
on

\mathfrak{R}_{I}

can be obtained as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{I}\leq B(\Delta_{\pi})w+C_{k}\sqrt{\frac{1}{w}\log{\left(\frac{\lambda+wH}{\lambda}\right)}}+C_{p}\sqrt{K-1}.

∎

Proof of Proposition
2
.

Now,
we
set the sliding window length
w

that
is adaptive to
\Delta_{\pi}
as follows
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{w}(\Delta_{\pi})=\left(\frac{C_{k}}{B(\Delta_{\pi})}\right)^{2/3}.

Then,

\displaystyle B(\Delta_{\pi})\widetilde{w}(\Delta_{\pi})+C_{k}\sqrt{\frac{1}{\widetilde{w}(\Delta_{\pi})}}\sqrt{\log{\left(\frac{\lambda+\widetilde{w}(\Delta_{\pi})H}{\lambda}\right)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{k}^{2/3}B(\Delta_{\pi})^{1/3}+C_{k}^{2/3}B(\Delta_{\pi})^{1/3}\sqrt{\log{\left(1+\frac{H}{\lambda}\left(\frac{C_{k}}{B(\Delta_{\pi})}\right)^{2/3}\right)}}.

Since
C_{k}
is linear to
K-1
, the function
\mathfrak{R}_{I}
satisfies that

\mathfrak{R}_{I}=\mathcal{O}\left(B(\Delta_{\pi})^{1/3}\left(K-1\right)^{2/3}\cdot\sqrt{\log{\left(\frac{K-1}{B(\Delta_{\pi})}\right)}}\right).

(D.61)

Now,
by utilizing
(
D.60
), if
B(\Delta_{\pi}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leq C_{B_{r}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{r}}+C_{B_{p}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{p}}}=o(K)
holds, then
\mathfrak{R}_{I}
is sublinear to
K
. The corresponding condition is
B_{r}(1)+\frac{\hat{r}_{\text{max}}}{1-\gamma}B_{p}(1)=o(K)

with

\Delta_{\pi}<K
since

\displaystyle C_{B_{r}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{r}}+C_{B_{p}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{p}}=o(K)

\displaystyle\left(C_{B_{r}}+C_{B_{p}}\right)\cdot\Delta_{\pi}^{\max\left(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p}\right)}=o(K)

\displaystyle\left(\left(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+H\right)B_{r}(1)+\left(\frac{1+H\hat{r}_{\text{max}}}{1-\gamma}+\right)B_{p}(1)\right)\cdot\Delta_{\pi}^{\max\left(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p}\right)}=o(K)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\left(B_{r}(1)+B_{p}(1)\right)+H\left(B_{r}(1)+\frac{\hat{r}_{\text{max}}}{1-\gamma}B_{p}(1)\right)\right)\cdot\Delta_{\pi}^{\max\left(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p}\right)}=o(K).

This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3
.

We first prove multiple statements below. We denote the upper bound
on

\mathfrak{R}_{I}
as
\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{I}
, and that of
\mathfrak{R}_{II}
as
\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{II}
.

1. The
upper bound on

\mathfrak{R}_{II}(\Delta_{\pi})

(i.e.,

\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{II}
) is a non-increasing function, the
upper bound on

\mathfrak{R}_{I}(\Delta_{\pi})

(i.e.,

\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{I}
) is
a
non-decreasing function , and both are convex in
the
region
\Delta_{\pi}\in\mathbb{N}_{I}\cap\mathbb{N}_{II}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{II}(\Delta_{\pi})}{\partial\Delta_{\pi}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial\Delta_{\pi}}\left(C_{1}(K-1)(\gamma+2)\left[(1-\eta\tau)^{\Delta_{\pi}-1}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\log{\left(1-\eta\tau\right)}C_{1}(K-1)(\gamma+2)\left[(1-\eta\tau)^{\Delta_{\pi}-1}\right]\leq 0

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{II}(\Delta_{\pi})}{\partial^{2}\Delta_{\pi}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial^{2}\Delta_{\pi}}\left(C_{1}(K-1)(\gamma+2)\left[(1-\eta\tau)^{\Delta_{\pi}-1}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\log{\left(1-\eta\tau\right)}\right)^{2}C_{1}(K-1)(\gamma+2)\left[(1-\eta\tau)^{\Delta_{\pi}-1}\right]\geq 0

since
\Delta_{\pi}\in\mathbb{N}_{I}\cap\mathbb{N}_{II}
satisfies
\Delta_{\pi}>1
and
\log(1-\eta\tau)\leq 0
holds
under
the hyperparameter assumption
0\leq\eta\leq(1-\gamma)/\tau
, it follows from
the Proposition
1

that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{I}(\Delta_{\pi})}{\partial\Delta_{\pi}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial\Delta_{\pi}}\left(C_{B_{r}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{r}}+C_{B_{p}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{p}}\right)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{r}C_{B_{r}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{r}-1}+\alpha_{p}C_{B_{p}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{p}-1}\geq 0

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{I}(\Delta_{\pi})}{\partial^{2}\Delta_{\pi}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial^{2}\Delta_{\pi}}\left(C_{B_{r}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{r}}+C_{B_{p}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{p}}\right)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{r}(\alpha_{r}-1)C_{B_{r}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{r}-2}+\alpha_{p}(\alpha_{p}-1)C_{B_{p}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{p}-2}\geq 0

when

\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p}\geq 1
.

2.
Suboptimal

\Delta^{*}_{\pi}

We
slightly relax the
upper bound

\mathfrak{R}_{I}(\Delta_{\pi}){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}~\leq~}C_{B_{r}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{r}}+C_{B_{p}}\Delta_{\pi}^{\alpha_{p}}
to
\mathfrak{R}_{I}(\Delta_{\pi})=\left(C_{B_{r}}+C_{B_{p}}\right)\Delta_{\pi}^{\max{\left(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p}\right)}}
and
obtain

\Delta_{\pi}^{*}

in
the worst case by optimizing
\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{I}(\Delta_{\pi})+\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{II}(\Delta_{\pi})
.

1.

\boldsymbol{\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}}=0
:
this means
that

\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{I}(\Delta_{\pi})=C_{B_{r}}+C_{B_{p}}
, where

\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{I}
is now independent
of

\Delta_{\pi}
. Then,
an infinite number

\Delta_{\pi}
guarantees
a
small dynamic regret
\mathfrak{R}_{I}
,
which
also leads to
a
small
\mathfrak{R}
.
It can be checked that

\mathfrak{R}_{II}
without entropy regularization decreases
with the scale of

1/\Delta_{\pi}
, and
\mathfrak{R}_{II}
with entropy regularization decreases
with the scale of

\exp{(\Delta_{\pi})}
. This also matches with the
existing results on achieving a faster convergence with an
entropy regularization
.

For the remaining case,
we
first compute the gradient of the term
\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{I}(\Delta_{\pi})+\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{II}(\Delta_{\pi})

when

\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{II}(\Delta_{\pi})
comes from entropy-regularized case
:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\left(\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{I}(\Delta_{\pi})+\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{II}(\Delta_{\pi})\right)}{\partial\Delta_{\pi}}

\displaystyle=\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}\left(\alpha_{r}C_{B_{r}}+\alpha_{p}C_{B_{p}}\right)\Delta_{\pi}^{\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}-1}-\log{\left(\frac{1}{1-\eta\tau}\right)}C_{1}(K-1)(\gamma+2)\left[(1-\eta\tau)^{\Delta_{\pi}-1}\right]

\displaystyle=k_{I}\Delta_{\pi}^{\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}-1}-k_{II}\left[(1-\eta\tau)^{\Delta_{\pi}-1}\right]

when

\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{II}(\Delta_{\pi})

is for the case without entropy regularization
, the gradient of
the
dynamic regret upper bound is given as

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\left(\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{I}(\Delta_{\pi})+\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{II}(\Delta_{\pi})\right)}{\partial\Delta_{\pi}}

\displaystyle=\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}\left(\alpha_{r}C_{B_{r}}+\alpha_{p}C_{B_{p}}\right)\Delta_{\pi}^{\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}-1}-\left(\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}+\frac{\log{|\mathcal{A}|}}{\eta}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\Delta_{\pi}^{2}}

\displaystyle=k_{I}\Delta_{\pi}^{\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}-1}-k_{II}\frac{1}{\Delta_{\pi}^{2}}

2.

\boldsymbol{\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}=1}
:
The relation

(1-\eta\tau)^{\Delta_{\pi}-1}=k_{I}/k_{II}
should be satisfied for
the
entropy regularized case and
\Delta_{\pi}^{-2}=k_{I}/k_{II}
should be satisfied
in the case without entropy regularization
, respectively. Then,
it holds that

\Delta_{\pi}^{*}=\log_{1-\eta\tau}(k_{I}/k_{II})+1
for
the
entropy regularized case and
\Delta_{\pi}^{*}=\sqrt{k_{II}/k_{I}}

without regularization
.

Now, for the case of
the
entropy regularized case, if
k_{II}=(1-\eta\tau)k_{I}
is satisfied,
\partial\left(\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{I}(\Delta_{\pi})+\mathfrak{R}^{\text{max}}_{II}(\Delta_{\pi})\right)/\partial\Delta_{\pi}=0
is equal to solving
\Delta_{\pi}^{\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}-1}=(1-\eta\tau)^{\Delta_{\pi}}
. Now, we use the Lambert W function to
find

\Delta_{\pi}
as follows
:

\displaystyle\Delta_{\pi}^{\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}-1}=(1-\eta\tau)^{\Delta_{\pi}}

\displaystyle\left(\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}-1\right)\log{\Delta_{\pi}}=\Delta_{\pi}\log{(1-\eta\tau)}

\displaystyle\Delta_{\pi}^{-1}\cdot\log{\Delta_{\pi}}=\frac{\log{(1-\eta\tau)}}{\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}-1}

\displaystyle-\log{\Delta_{\pi}}\cdot e^{-\log{\Delta_{\pi}}}=-\frac{\log{(1-\eta\tau)}}{\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}-1}

\displaystyle W\left[-\log{\Delta_{\pi}}\cdot e^{-\log{\Delta_{\pi}}}\right]=W\left[-\frac{\log{(1-\eta\tau)}}{\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}-1}\right]

\displaystyle W\left[-\log{\Delta_{\pi}}\cdot e^{-\log{G}}\right]=W\left[-\frac{\log{(1-\eta\tau)}}{\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}-1}\right]

\displaystyle-\log{\Delta_{\pi}}=W\left[-\frac{\log{(1-\eta\tau)}}{\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}-1}\right]

\displaystyle\Delta_{\pi}^{*}=\exp{\left(-W\left[-\frac{\log{(1-\eta\tau)}}{\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}-1}\right]\right)}=\exp{\left(-W\left[x\right]\right)}

3.

\boldsymbol{0<\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}<1}
:

•

Without Entropy-regularization:
\Delta_{\pi}^{*}=(k_{I}/k_{II})^{1/(\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}+1)}

•

With Entropy-regularization:
Since

x=-\frac{\log{(1-\eta\tau)}}{\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}-1}<0
,
a
small
|x|

will have a
large
-W(x)>0
value, which leads to
a large

\Delta_{\pi}^{*}
.

4.

\boldsymbol{\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}>1}
:

•

Without Entropy-regularization:
\Delta_{\pi}^{*}=(k_{I}/k_{II})^{1/(\max{(\alpha_{r},\alpha_{p})}+1)}

•

With Entropy-regularization:
It holds that

x>0
and
-W(x)<0
. Then
\Delta_{\pi}^{*}<1
, which
means
that one
iteration is enough.

∎

From the proof of
Theorem

2
, we will develop Lemma
4
, Lemma
5

and
Lemma

to upper-bound
two model prediction errors
-\iota^{(k)}_{h}

and

\bar{\iota}_{\infty}^{k}
.

-\iota^{(k+1)}_{h}(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})\leq\bar{\Delta}^{r}_{k,h}+2\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)+\gamma\bar{\Delta}^{p}_{k,h}\frac{\gamma^{H-h}\hat{r}_{\text{max}}}{1-\gamma}

Lemma 5

(Upper bound
on

-\iota^{(k+1)}_{h}(s,a)
by
\Delta^{r}_{(k)},~\Delta^{p}_{(k)}
)
.

For
every

(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
,
it holds that

-\iota^{(k+1)}_{h}(s,a)\leq\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)+\gamma\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)\frac{\gamma^{H-h}\hat{r}_{\text{max}}}{1-\gamma}+2\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)

Proof of Lemma
5
.

\displaystyle-\iota^{(k+1)}_{h}(s,a)=

\displaystyle-R_{(k+1)}(s,a)-\gamma(P_{(k+1)}\widehat{V}^{\widehat{\pi}^{(k+1)},(k+1)}_{h+1})(s,a)+\widehat{Q}^{\widehat{\pi}^{(k+1)},(k+1)}_{h}(s,a)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-R_{(k+1)}(s,a)-\gamma(P_{(k+1)}\widehat{V}^{\widehat{\pi}^{(k+1)},(k+1)}_{h+1})(s,a)

\displaystyle+\widehat{R}_{(k+1)}(s,a)+\gamma(\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}\widehat{V}^{\widehat{\pi}^{(k+1)},(k+1)}_{h+1})(s,a)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{R}_{(k+1)}-R_{(k+1)}\right)(s,a)+\gamma\left(\left(\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}-P_{(k+1)}\right)\widehat{V}^{\widehat{\pi}^{(k+1)},(k+1)}_{h+1}\right)(s,a)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)+2\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)+\gamma\left|\left|\left(\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}-P_{(k+1)}\right)(\cdot~|~s,a)\right|\right|_{1}\left|\left|\widehat{V}^{\widehat{\pi}^{(k+1)},(k+1)}_{h+1}(\cdot)\right|\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)+2\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)+\gamma\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)\frac{\gamma^{H-h}\hat{r}_{\text{max}}}{1-\gamma}

.

(

),

\displaystyle\bar{\iota}^{k+1}_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left(R_{(k+1)}-\widetilde{R}_{(k+1)}\right)-\beta(\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))^{1/2}+\gamma\left(P_{(k+1)}-\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}\right)\widehat{V}^{\bigast,(k+1)}_{\infty}-\beta(\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|R_{(k+1)}-\widetilde{R}_{(k+1)}\right|-\beta(\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))^{1/2}+\gamma\left|\left|P_{(k+1)}-\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}\right|\right|_{1}\left|\left|\widehat{V}^{\bigast,(k+1)}_{\infty}\right|\right|_{\infty}-\beta(\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)-\beta(\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))^{1/2}+\gamma\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)\frac{\hat{r}_{max}}{1-\gamma}-\beta(\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))^{1/2}

Lemma 7

(Upper bound
on

\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)
)
.

For
every

(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
,
it holds that

\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)\leq B^{(k-w:k)}_{r}(\Delta_{\pi})+\lambda\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)r_{\text{max}}

Proof of Lemma
7
.

We directly utilize the proof of
Lemma 35 in

[
31
]
.
For every

(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
,
\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)
can be represented as

\displaystyle\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)

(D.62)

\displaystyle=|R_{(k+1)}(s,a)-\widetilde{R}_{(k+1)}(s,a)|

(D.63)

\displaystyle=|o^{r}_{(k+1)}(s,a)-\widetilde{o}^{r}_{(k+1)}(s,a)|

(D.64)

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{\sum_{t=(1\wedge k-w+1)}^{k}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]\cdot r^{t}_{h}}{\lambda+\sum_{t=(1\wedge k-w+1)}^{k}n_{t}(s,a)}-o^{r}_{(k+1)}(s,a)\right|

(D.65)

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\left|\sum_{t=(1\wedge k-w+1)}^{k}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]\cdot r^{t}_{h}-\left(\lambda+\sum_{t=(1\wedge k-w+1)}^{k}n_{t}(s,a)\right)o^{r}_{(k+1)}(s,a)\right|

(D.66)

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\left|\sum_{t=(1\wedge k-w+1)}^{k}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left(\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]\left(r^{t}_{h}-o^{r}_{(k+1)}(s,a)\right)\right)-\lambda\cdot o^{r}_{(k+1)}(s,a)\right|

(D.67)

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\left(\sum_{t=(1\wedge k-w+1)}^{k}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]\cdot\left|r^{t}_{h}-o^{r}_{(k+1)}(s,a)\right|\right)+\lambda\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\left|o^{r}_{(k+1)}(s,a)\right|

(D.69)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{(1\wedge k-w+1)\leq t\leq k}\left(\left|r^{t}(s,a)-o^{r}_{(k+1)}(s,a)\right|\right)\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\left(\sum_{t=(1\wedge k-w+1)}^{k}n_{t}(s,a)\right)+\lambda\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)r_{\text{max}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{(1\wedge k-w+1)\leq t\leq k}\left(\left|r^{t}(s,a)-o^{r}_{(k+1)}(s,a)\right|\right)+\lambda\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)r_{\text{max}}

(D.70)

,

) holds since

.

Lemma 8

(Upper bound
on

Proof of Lemma

.

The total empirical forecasting model error up to

is given as

\displaystyle\bar{\Delta}^{r}_{K}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\bar{\Delta}^{r}_{k,h}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})

(D.71)

(D.72)

inequality
(

, the equation (

\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}B^{(k-w:k)}_{r}(\Delta_{\pi})=\sum_{\mathcal{E}=1}^{{\left\lfloor\frac{K-1}{w}\right\rfloor}}\sum_{k=(\mathcal{E}-1)w}^{\mathcal{E}w}B^{(k-w:k)}_{r}(\Delta_{\pi})\leq wB_{r}(\Delta_{\pi})

.

Lemma 9

(Upper bound
on
the term
\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sqrt{\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})}
)
.

It holds that

\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left(\sqrt{\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})}\right)\leq(K-1)\sqrt{\frac{H}{w}}\sqrt{\log{\left(\frac{\lambda+wH}{\lambda}\right)}}

Proof of lemma
9
.

We denote
\bar{\Lambda}_{w}^{k}=\lambda\mathbb{I}+\sum_{t=(1\wedge k-w+1)}^{k}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\varphi(s_{h}^{t},a_{h}^{t})\varphi(s_{h}^{t},a_{h}^{t})^{\top}
. Also, we denote
(\bar{\Lambda}_{w}^{k})^{(1)}=\lambda\mathbb{I}+\varphi(s_{h}^{(1\wedge k-w+1)},a_{h}^{(1\wedge k-w+1)})\varphi(s_{h}^{(1\wedge k-w+1)},a_{h}^{(1\wedge k-w+1)})^{\top}
Then, for
every

(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
,
\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)=\varphi(s,a)(\bar{\Lambda}^{k}_{w})^{-1}\varphi(s,a)^{\top}
holds. Now, the following term can be bounded
as

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sqrt{\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sqrt{\varphi(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})(\bar{\Lambda}^{k}_{w})^{-1}\varphi(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})^{\top}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathcal{E}=1}^{\left\lfloor\frac{K-1}{w}\right\rfloor}\sum_{k=(\mathcal{E}-1)w+1}^{\mathcal{E}w}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\sqrt{\varphi(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})(\bar{\Lambda}^{k}_{w})^{-1}\varphi(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})^{\top}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathcal{E}=1}^{\left\lfloor\frac{K-1}{w}\right\rfloor}\sqrt{Hw}\sqrt{\sum_{k=(\mathcal{E}-1)w+1}^{\mathcal{E}w}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\varphi(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})(\bar{\Lambda}^{k}_{w})^{-1}\varphi(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})^{\top}}

(D.74)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathcal{E}=1}^{\left\lfloor\frac{K-1}{w}\right\rfloor}\sqrt{Hw}\sqrt{\log{\left(\frac{\text{det}\left(\Lambda^{\mathcal{E}w+1}_{w}\right)}{\text{det}\left((\Lambda^{(\mathcal{E}-1)w+2}_{w})^{(1)}\right)}\right)}}

(D.75)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lfloor\frac{K-1}{w}\right\rfloor\sqrt{Hw}\sqrt{\log{\left(\frac{\lambda+wH}{\lambda}\right)}}

(D.76)

\displaystyle\leq(K-1)\sqrt{\frac{H}{w}}\sqrt{\log{\left(\frac{\lambda+wH}{\lambda}\right)}}

The inequality
(
D.74
) holds by
the
Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, (
D.75
) holds by
Lemmas
(D.1) and (D.2)
in

[
39
]
,
and
(
D.76
) holds since
(\Lambda^{(\mathcal{E}-1)w+2}_{w})^{(1)}\geq\lambda

and

\Lambda^{\mathcal{E}w+1}_{w}\leq\lambda+wH
.
∎

Lemma 10

(Upper bound
on

\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)
)
.

For
every

(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
and given
\delta\in(0,1)
, the following holds with probability
at least

1-\delta
:

\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)\leq B^{(k-w+1:k)}_{p}+(\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))^{1/2}\cdot|\mathcal{S}|\cdot\sqrt{\frac{H^{2}}{2}\log{\left(\frac{H}{\delta\lambda}\right)}}+\lambda\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)

Proof of lemma
10
.

For
every

(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
,
one can write:

\displaystyle\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)

\displaystyle=||P_{(k+1)}(\cdot|s,a)-\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}(\cdot|s,a)||_{1}

\displaystyle=||o^{p}_{(k+1)}(\cdot,s,a)-\widehat{o}^{p}_{(k+1)}(\cdot,s,a)||_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\left|\frac{\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}n_{t}(s^{\prime},s,a)}{\lambda+\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}n_{t}(s,a)}-o^{p}_{(k+1)}(s^{\prime},s,a)\right|

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\left|\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}n_{t}(s^{\prime},s,a)-\left(\lambda+\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}n_{t}(s,a)\right)o^{p}_{(k+1)}(s^{\prime},s,a)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\left(\left|\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}\left(n_{t}(s^{\prime},s,a)-n_{t}(s,a)o^{p}_{(k+1)}(s^{\prime},s,a)\right)\right|+\left|\lambda o^{p}_{(k+1)}(s^{\prime},s,a)\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\left|\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}\left(n_{t}(s^{\prime},s,a)-n_{t}(s,a)o^{p}_{(k+1)}(s^{\prime},s,a)\right)\right|+\lambda\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)

(D.77)

Recall that
n_{t}(s^{\prime},s,a),~n_{t}(s,a)
is defined as

\displaystyle n_{t}(s^{\prime},s,a)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s^{\prime},s,a)=(s^{t}_{h+1},s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]\cdot\mathbbm{1}\left[s^{\prime}=s^{t}_{h+1}\right]

(D.78)

\displaystyle n_{t}(s,a)

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]

(D.79)

where
\mathbbm{1}[\cdot]
is an indicator function.
Substituting
(
D.78
)
and
(
D.79
) into (
D.77
)
yields that

\displaystyle\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\left|\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}\left(n_{t}(s^{\prime},s,a)-n_{t}(s,a)o^{p}_{(k+1)}(s^{\prime},s,a)\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\left|\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]\cdot\mathbbm{1}\left[s^{\prime}=s^{t}_{h+1}\right]\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.-\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]\cdot o^{p}_{(k+1)}(s^{\prime},s,a)\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\left|\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]\left(\mathbbm{1}\left[s^{\prime}=s^{t}_{h+1}\right]-o^{p}_{(k+1)}(s^{\prime},s,a)\right)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\bigg|\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]\left(\mathbbm{1}\left[s^{\prime}=s^{t}_{h+1}\right]-o^{p}_{t}(s^{\prime},s,a)\right)\right)\bigg|}_{\hbox to12.91pt{\vbox to12.91pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.45293pt\lower-6.45293pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-5.11807pt}{-2.25555pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\bigg|\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]\left(o^{p}_{t}(s^{\prime},s,a)-o^{p}_{(k+1)}(s^{\prime},s,a)\right)\right)\bigg|}_{\hbox to12.91pt{\vbox to12.91pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 6.45293pt\lower-6.45293pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 8.65 0 C 8.65 4.78 4.78 8.65 0 8.65 C -4.78 8.65 -8.65 4.78 -8.65 0 C -8.65 -4.78 -4.78 -8.65 0 -8.65 C 4.78 -8.65 8.65 -4.78 8.65 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-5.11807pt}{-2.25555pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}

The term
2.1
can be upperbounded by utilizing the
Lemmas (34) and (43)
in
[
31
]
.
For every

t\in[K]

and

s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
,
we define the
random variable
\eta^{t}(s^{\prime})\coloneqq\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left(\mathbbm{1}\left[s^{\prime}=s^{t}_{h+1}\right]-o^{p}_{t}(s^{\prime},s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right)
.

Given

s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
, the
sequence
\{\eta^{\tau}(s^{\prime})\}_{\tau=1}^{\infty}
is
a
zero-mean and
H/2
-sub Gaussian random variable.
From the
Lemma 43 in

[
31
]
,
we set

Y=\lambda\mathbb{I}

and

X_{t}=\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]
. Then, for
a
given
\delta\in(0,1)
, the following holds with probability
at least

1-\delta
for
all

(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle\left|(\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))^{1/2}\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}\left(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]\cdot\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}[s^{\prime}=s^{t}_{h+1}]-o_{t}^{p}(s^{\prime},s,a)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{H^{2}}{2}\log{\left(\frac{(\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))^{-1/2}\cdot\lambda^{-1/2}}{\delta/H}\right)}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{H^{2}}{2}\log{\left(\frac{H}{\delta}\cdot\frac{1}{(\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))^{1/2}\cdot\lambda^{1/2}}\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{H^{2}}{2}\log{\left(\frac{H}{\delta}\cdot\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)}}

(D.80)

As a result, the following inequality
holds with
probability at least

1-\delta
:

2.1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))^{1/2}\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\bigg|(\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))^{1/2}\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}\bigg(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]\cdot\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}[s^{\prime}=s^{t}_{h+1}]

\displaystyle-o_{t}^{p}(s^{\prime},s,a)\bigg)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))^{1/2}\cdot|\mathcal{S}|\cdot\sqrt{\frac{H^{2}}{2}\log{\left(\frac{H}{\delta\lambda}\right)}}

The term
2.2
can be bounded
as

2.2

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]\bigg|o^{p}_{t}(s^{\prime},s,a)-o^{p}_{(k+1)}(s^{\prime},s,a)\bigg|

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\bigg|o^{p}_{t}(s^{\prime},s,a)-o^{p}_{(k+1)}(s^{\prime},s,a)\bigg|

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]\bigg|\bigg|o^{p}_{t}(\cdot,s,a)-o^{p}_{(k+1)}(\cdot,s,a)\bigg|\bigg|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{t\in[k-w+1,k]}\left(\bigg|\bigg|o^{p}_{t}(\cdot,s,a)-o^{p}_{(k+1)}(\cdot,s,a)\bigg|\bigg|_{1}\right)\cdot\left(\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\sum_{t=k-w+1}^{k}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbbm{1}\left[(s,a)=(s^{t}_{h},a^{t}_{h})\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{t\in[k-w+1,k]}\left(\bigg|\bigg|o^{p}_{t}(\cdot,s,a)-o^{p}_{(k+1)}(\cdot,s,a)\bigg|\bigg|_{1}\right)\cdot 1

\displaystyle\leq B^{(k-w+1:k)}_{p}(\Delta_{\pi})

(D.81)

Then,
by combining
(
D.77
), (
D.80
)
and
(
D.81
), the term
\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)

can be expressed as

\displaystyle\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)\leq B^{(k-w+1:k)}_{p}(\Delta_{\pi})+(\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))^{1/2}\cdot|\mathcal{S}|\cdot\sqrt{\frac{H^{2}}{2}\log{\left(\frac{H}{\delta\lambda}\right)}}+\lambda\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s,a).

∎

Lemma 11

(Upper bound
on

\bar{\Delta}^{p}_{K}
)
.

Given

\delta\in(0,1)
, the following
inequality holds
with
probability at least

1-\delta
:

\bar{\Delta}^{p}_{K}\leq\left(|\mathcal{S}|\sqrt{\frac{H^{2}}{2}\log{\left(\frac{H}{\delta\lambda}\right)}}+\lambda\right)(K-1)\sqrt{\frac{H}{w}}\sqrt{\log{\left(\frac{\lambda+wH}{\lambda}\right)}}+wHB_{p}(\Delta_{\pi})

Proof of lemma
11
.

The total empirical forecasting transition probability model error
\bar{\Delta}^{p}_{K}
can be represented as follows,

\displaystyle\bar{\Delta}^{p}_{K}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\bar{\Delta}^{p}_{k,h}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left((\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h}))^{1/2}|\mathcal{S}|\sqrt{\frac{H^{2}}{2}\log{\left(\frac{H}{\delta\lambda}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left(\max_{t\in[k-w+1,k]}\bigg|\bigg|o^{p}_{t}(\cdot,s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})-o^{p}_{(k+1)}(\cdot,s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})\bigg|\bigg|_{1}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left(\lambda\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(|\mathcal{S}|\sqrt{\frac{H^{2}}{2}\log{\left(\frac{H}{\delta\lambda}\right)}}+\lambda\right)\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left((\Lambda^{(k)}_{w}(s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h}))^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left(\max_{t\in[k-w+1,k]}\bigg|\bigg|o^{p}_{t}(\cdot,s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})-o^{p}_{(k+1)}(\cdot,s^{(k+1)}_{h},a^{(k+1)}_{h})\bigg|\bigg|_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(|\mathcal{S}|\sqrt{\frac{H^{2}}{2}\log{\left(\frac{H}{\delta\lambda}\right)}}+\lambda\right)(K-1)\sqrt{\frac{H}{w}}\sqrt{\log{\left(\frac{\lambda+wH}{\lambda}\right)}}+wHB_{p}(\Delta_{\pi})

∎

Proof of Theorem
4
.

Before introduing the proof, we first go over some details about Theorem
4
in the following paragraph.

The
W-LSE
involves solving
the
following joint optimization problem over
\phi^{r}_{f}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|},\phi^{p}_{f}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|^{2}|\mathcal{A}|}

and

q\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
to obtain a minimum upper bound
on
the dynamic regret:

\displaystyle\min_{\phi^{\diamond}_{f},q}\mathcal{L}\left(\phi^{\diamond}_{f},q~;~\square_{1:N}\right)\text{ where }\mathcal{L}\left(\phi^{\diamond}_{f},q~;~\square_{1:N}\right)=\sum_{t=1}^{N}q_{t}\left(\widehat{\square}_{\phi^{\diamond}_{f}}^{k+1}-\square_{t}\right)^{2}+\text{disc}(q)+\frac{1}{wH}\cdot\lambda||\phi^{\diamond}_{f}||_{2}

(D.82)

where
\diamond=r\text{ or }p
. If
\diamond=r
, then
\square=R(s,a)
and if
\diamond=p
, then
\square=P(s^{\prime},s,a)
.
Moreover,

{\square}_{\phi^{\diamond}_{f}}
means that
\square
is parameterized by
\phi^{\diamond}_{f}
, and
\square_{1:N}
are observed data of
\square
, and the disc
(q)\coloneqq\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\left(\mathbb{E}[f(\widehat{\square}^{k+1}~|~\square_{1:N}]-\sum_{t=1}^{N}q_{t}\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\square}^{t}|\square_{1:t-1}]\right)
measures the non-stationarity of the environment. disc
(q)
could be measured and
upper-bounded
by
the
observed data. For example, if
\diamond=r
and
\square=R
, then

\phi^{r}_{f}
parameterizes the future reward function
\widehat{R}_{\phi^{r}_{f}}^{k+1}
,
N
is the total number of visits of
(s,a)
up to episode
k
,
R_{1:N}(s,a)
is the set of reward values
\{R_{1}(s,a),R_{1}(s,a),\dots,R_{N}(s,a)\}
that the agent has received when visiting
(s,a)
. We demonstrate
a
modified upper bound
on

\mathfrak{R}_{I}
when utilizing
W-LSE
.
To do so
, we define
the
forecasting reward model error
\Delta_{r,k}^{1}(s,a)=\left|\left(R_{(k+1)}-\widetilde{R}_{(k+1)}\right)(s,a)\right|

and the
forecasting transition probability model error as
\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)=\left|\left|\left(P_{(k+1)}-\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}\right)(\cdot~|~s,a)\right|\right|_{1}
where
\widetilde{R}_{(k+1)}
and
\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}
are predicted reward,transition probability from function
g\circ f
(Appendix
D.2
).

We now
brought
the
Theorem
7 of
[
22
]
to offer
an
upper bound
on
the
l_{2}
-norm of the reward gap between
R_{(k+1)}(s,a)
and
\widetilde{R}_{(k+1)}(s,a)
as follows.
To this end, we
denote
X_{k,h}=(s^{(k)}_{h},a^{(k)}_{h})\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A},~Y_{k,h}=R_{(k)}(s^{(k)}_{h},a^{(k)}_{h})\in\mathbb{R}
and assume
that the
environment
provides the agent with a
noisy reward
\widehat{Y}_{k,h}=Y_{k,h}+\eta
, where
\eta
is sampled from a zero-mean Gaussian.
Define the kernel
\Psi(x)=\varphi(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
, where

\varphi(x)
is the one-hot vector that we have defined
in Section

D.1.1
. Now,
we set

r(x)=c^{\top}\varphi(x)
where the vector
c\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
is
the
same as the estimated future reward vector
\widetilde{R}_{(k+1)}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
and
r(x)
is
the
same as the estimated future reward when
x=(s,a)
, namely
\widetilde{R}_{(k+1)}(s,a)
. Then, for data until episode
k
, i.e.,
\mathcal{D}_{data}=\{(X_{1,0},\widehat{Y}_{1,0}),(X_{1,1},\widehat{Y}_{1,1}),..,(X_{k,H-1},\widehat{Y}_{k,H-1})\}
, we denote
\mathcal{D}^{(s,a)}_{data}\coloneqq\{(X_{k,h},\widehat{Y}_{k,h})~|~X_{k,h}=(s,a)\text{ such that }(X_{k,h},\widehat{Y}_{k,h})\in\mathcal{D}_{data}\}
. We relabel
\mathcal{D}^{(s,a)}_{data}
as
\{\left((s,a),\widehat{Y}_{1}\right),\left((s,a),\widehat{Y}_{2}\right),...,\left((s,a),\widehat{Y}_{N}\right)\}

such that

N(s,a)=\sum_{t=1}^{k}n_{t}(s,a)
is the total number of visitations of
(s,a)
until episode
k
(Definition (
D.79
)). We
use the shorthand notation

N
as
N(s,a)
, and
\sum_{t=1}^{N}q_{t}=1
.
For every

(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
, the following
inequalities hold
with probability at least
1-\delta
for all functions
r\in\{x\rightarrow c^{\top}\Psi(x):||c||_{2}\leq\Lambda\}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\left(r(s,a)-\widehat{Y}_{N+1}\right)^{2}|\mathcal{D}^{(s,a)}_{data}]\leq\sum_{t=1}^{N}q_{t}\left(r(s,a)-\widehat{Y}_{t}\right)^{2}+\text{disc}(q)+\frac{1}{wH}\cdot\lambda||\bar{r}||_{2}

(D.83)

Take the expectation
over
\eta
on both inequailty.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left(r(s,a)-\widehat{Y}_{N+1}\right)^{2}|\mathcal{D}^{(s,a)}_{data}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{N}q_{t}\left(r(s,a)-\widehat{Y}_{t}\right)^{2}+\text{disc}(q)+\frac{1}{wH}\cdot\lambda||\bar{r}||_{2}\right],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(r(s,a)-\widehat{Y}_{N+1}\right)^{2}|\mathcal{D}^{(s,a)}_{data}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left[q_{t}\left(r(s,a)-\widehat{Y}_{t}\right)^{2}\right]+\text{disc}(q)+\frac{1}{wH}\cdot\lambda||\bar{r}||_{2}.

The
left-hand side
of (
D.83
) can be expressed
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(r(s,a)-\widehat{Y}_{N+1}-\eta)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left[(r(s,a)-Y_{N+1})^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left[\eta^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=(r(s,a)-Y_{N+1})^{2}+\mathbb{E}\left[\eta^{2}\right]

(D.84)

Also, the
term
\sum_{t=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left[q_{t}\left(r(s,a)-\widehat{Y}_{t}\right)^{2}\right]
of the
right-hand side
of equation (
D.83
)
can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\eta}[q_{t}\left(r(s,a)-\widehat{Y}_{t}\right)^{2}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\eta}[q_{t}\left(\left(r(s,a)-Y_{t}\right)^{2}+\eta^{2}\right)]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left[q_{t}\left(\left(r(s,a)-Y_{t}\right)^{2}\right)\right]+\sum_{t=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left[q_{t}\eta^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{N}q_{t}\left(\left(r(s,a)-Y_{t}\right)^{2}\right)+\mathbb{E}_{\eta}\left[\eta^{2}\right]

By eliminating

\mathbb{E}_{\eta}[\eta^{2}]

from both sides, we obtain that

\displaystyle(r(s,a)-Y_{N+1})^{2}\leq\sum_{t=1}^{N}q_{t}\left(\left(r(s,a)-Y_{t}\right)^{2}\right)+\text{disc}(q)+\frac{1}{wH}\cdot\lambda||\bar{r}||_{2}

(D.85)

Recall the definition of
r(s,a)=\widetilde{R}_{(k+1)}(s,a)
,
Y_{t}=R_{t}(s,a)
. Since
t

matches
one of
(k,h)\in[K]\times[H]
pairs, we can rewrite

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{N}q_{t}\left(r(s,a)-\widehat{Y}_{t}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}q_{(k^{\prime},h)}\left(r(s,a)-Y_{(k,h)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}q_{(k^{\prime},h)}\left(\widetilde{R}_{(k+1)}(s,a)-R^{k^{\prime}}_{h}(s,a)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}q_{(k^{\prime},h)}\left(\widetilde{R}_{(k+1)}(s,a)-R^{k^{\prime}}(s,a)\right)^{2}

where if
(s,a)
is not visited at step
h
of episode
k
, then
the
corresponding
q_{(k^{\prime},h)}
is zero.
As a result,

\displaystyle\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\min_{q,\bar{r}}\left(\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}q_{(k^{\prime},h)}\left(\widetilde{R}_{(k+1)}(s,a)-R^{k^{\prime}}(s,a)\right)^{2}+\text{disc}(q)+\frac{1}{wH}\cdot\lambda||\bar{r}||_{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\min_{q,\bar{r}}\left(\left(\max_{1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k}\left(\widetilde{R}_{(k+1)}(s,a)-R^{k^{\prime}}(s,a)\right)\right)^{2}\left(\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}q_{(k^{\prime},h)}\right)+\text{disc}(q)+\frac{1}{wH}\cdot\lambda||\bar{r}||_{2}\right)}

A similar analysis for

\Delta^{p}_{(k)}

leads to the following inequality for all

(s^{\prime},s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle\left|P_{(k+1)}(s^{\prime}~|~s,a)-\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}(s^{\prime}~|~s,a)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\min_{q,\bar{p}}\left(\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}q_{(k^{\prime},h)}\left(\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}(s^{\prime}|s,a)-P^{k^{\prime}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\right)^{2}+\text{disc}(q)+\frac{1}{wH}\cdot\lambda||\bar{p}||_{2}\right)}

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sqrt{\min_{q,\bar{p}}\left(\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}q_{(k^{\prime},h)}\left(\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}(s^{\prime}|s,a)-P^{k^{\prime}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\right)^{2}+\text{disc}(q)+\frac{1}{wH}\cdot\lambda||\bar{p}||_{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{S}|\sqrt{\min_{q,\bar{p}}\left(\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}q_{(k^{\prime},h)}\left|\left|\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}(\cdot|s,a)-P^{k^{\prime}}(\cdot|s,a)\right|\right|_{\infty}^{2}+\text{disc}(q)+\frac{1}{wH}\cdot\lambda||\bar{p}||_{2}\right)}

Recall the Corollary
5
, Corollary
6
and
\mathfrak{R}_{I}
definition.
Aftering fixing

(s,a)
,
the term

\mathfrak{R}_{I}(s,a)

can be expressed as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{I}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\bar{\iota}_{\infty}^{k+1}+\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}-\iota^{(k+1)}_{h}+C_{p}\sqrt{K-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\left(\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)+\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)\frac{\gamma\hat{r}_{max}}{1-\gamma}-2\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left(\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)+\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)\frac{\gamma\hat{r}_{max}}{1-\gamma}+2\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\right)

\displaystyle+C_{p}\sqrt{K-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\left(\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)+\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\left(\tilde{r}_{\text{max}}+\max(2\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))\right)-2\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\right)

\displaystyle+H\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\left(\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)+\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\left(\tilde{r}_{\text{max}}+\max(2\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))\right)+2\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)\right)

\displaystyle+C_{p}\sqrt{K-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\bigg(\underbrace{\left(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+H\right)\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)+\frac{\gamma\tilde{r}_{\text{max}}}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+H\right)\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)}_{\hbox to7.83pt{\vbox to7.83pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.91264pt\lower-3.91264pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 5.14 0 C 5.14 2.84 2.84 5.14 0 5.14 C -2.84 5.14 -5.14 2.84 -5.14 0 C -5.14 -2.84 -2.84 -5.14 0 -5.14 C 2.84 -5.14 5.14 -2.84 5.14 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.99306pt}{-2.25555pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}+

\displaystyle+\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+H\right)\max(2\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)\bigg)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}2\left(-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+H\right)\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)

\displaystyle+C_{p}\sqrt{K-1}

We set
the term
1

to be

2(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+H)\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)
, which requires redefining
the exploration bonus term
as

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)=\frac{1}{2}\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)+\frac{\gamma\tilde{r}_{\text{max}}}{2(1-\gamma)}\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a).

Also, note that
\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)=\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\left|\left(\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}-P_{(k+1)}\right)(s^{\prime}|s,a)\right|\leq\left|\mathcal{S}\right|
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{I}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\left(4H\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a)+\frac{2\gamma}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+H\right)\max(\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))\left|\mathcal{S}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\left(4H+\frac{2\gamma}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+H\right)\right)\max(\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))

\displaystyle=\left(4H+\frac{2\gamma\left|\mathcal{S}\right|}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+H\right)\right)\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\max(\Gamma^{(k)}_{w}(s,a))

\displaystyle\leq\left(4H+\frac{2\gamma\left|\mathcal{S}\right|}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+H\right)\right)\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}\max(\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a))+\frac{\gamma\tilde{r}_{\text{max}}}{2(1-\gamma)}\max(\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a))\right)

\displaystyle=\left(4H+\frac{2\gamma\left|\mathcal{S}\right|}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+H\right)\right)\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)+\frac{\gamma\tilde{r}_{\text{max}}}{2(1-\gamma)}\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)\right)

\displaystyle=\left(4H+\frac{2\gamma\left|\mathcal{S}\right|}{1-\gamma}\left(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+H\right)\right)\left(\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)+\frac{\gamma\tilde{r}_{\text{max}}}{2(1-\gamma)}\sum_{k=1}^{K-1}\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)\right)

Note that above upper bound
on

\mathfrak{R}_{I}
holds
under the following conditions for

\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)

and

\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)
:

\displaystyle\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\min_{q,\bar{r}}\left(\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}q_{(k^{\prime},h)}\left(\widetilde{R}_{(k+1)}(s,a)-R^{k^{\prime}}(s,a)\right)^{2}+\text{disc}(q)+\frac{1}{wH}\cdot\lambda||\bar{r}||_{2}\right)},

\displaystyle\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}}\sqrt{\min_{q,\bar{p}}\left(\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}q_{(k^{\prime},h)}\left(\widehat{P}_{(k+1)}(s^{\prime}|s,a)-P^{k^{\prime}}(s^{\prime}|s,a)\right)^{2}+\text{disc}(q)+\frac{1}{wH}\cdot\lambda||\bar{p}||_{2}\right)}.

∎

Proof of Remark
1
.

The proof starts
with
(
D.85
).
Define

\displaystyle q^{sw}_{t}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{wH}&\text{if }t\in(k-w,k]\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

\displaystyle r^{sw}

\displaystyle=\argmin_{\bar{r}}\left(\lambda||\bar{r}||^{2}+\sum_{t=1}^{N}\left(r(s,a)-\widehat{Y}_{t}\right)^{2}\right).

(D.86)

where
r_{sw}
is the same reward estimation
as in
(
D.7
). Then the minimum
of (
D.83
)
yields that

\displaystyle\min_{\bar{r},q}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{N}q_{t}\left(r(s,a)-\widehat{Y}_{t}\right)^{2}+\text{disc}(q)+\frac{1}{wH}\cdot\lambda||\bar{r}||_{2}\right)

(D.87)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{\bar{r}}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{N}q^{sw}_{t}\left(r(s,a)-\widehat{Y}_{t}\right)^{2}+\text{disc}(q^{sw})+\frac{1}{Hw}\cdot\lambda||\bar{r}||_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{Hw}\underbrace{\min_{\bar{r}}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{N}(Hw)\cdot q^{sw}_{t}\left(r(s,a)-\widehat{Y}_{t}\right)^{2}+\lambda||\bar{r}||_{2}\right)}_{\hbox to7.83pt{\vbox to7.83pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.91264pt\lower-3.91264pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.99306pt}{-2.25555pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}+~\text{disc}(q_{sw}).

(D.88)

The term
1
is the optimization problem
in
(
D.86
)
whose
minimizer is
r^{sw}
.
An
inspection
of
(
D.87
)
and
(
D.88
)
concludes
that the
optimal solution
(
q^{*},\bar{r}^{*}
),
namely the
minimizer of (
D.87
)
provides a
smaller value than
(q^{sw},r^{sw})
. Since the
right-hand side
(
D.85
) is same
as
(
D.87
),
(q^{*},\bar{r}^{*})

provides
a tighter upper bound
on the left-hand side
term of equation (
D.83
) than
q^{sw},r^{sw}
.
Therefore, (
D.84
)
implies
that the optimal
solution

(q^{*},\bar{r}^{*})
gives a tighter upper bound
on

\Delta^{r}_{(k)}

than using

(q^{sw},\bar{r}^{sw})
.
One can repeat the above argument
for the upper bound
on

\Delta^{p}_{(k)}
. Then, by Corollary
5
and
6
, the tighter upper bound
s on

\Delta^{r}_{(k)}(s,a)

and

\Delta^{p}_{(k)}(s,a)

provide
smaller upper
bounds on

-\iota^{(k)}_{H},\bar{\iota}^{K}_{\infty}
and
lead to
a tighter upper bound on
\mathfrak{R}_{I}
.
∎

Appendix E
Experimental design and results

E.1
Environment setting details

Reward function design.

All three environments share the same reward function structure and have an identical goal. The reward function
R
consists of three parts
R=R_{h}+R_{f}-R_{c}
, where

R_{h}
is the healthy reward,
R_{f}=k_{f}(x_{t+1}-x_{t})/\Delta t,k_{f}>0
is the forward reward, and
R_{c}
is the control cost.
The agents
have a goal to run faster in the
+x
direction,
and therefore
the faster they run, the higher the forward reward
R_{f}

is
. We
modify
the environment to make the agent’s desired directions change as the episode goes by. To be specific, we
design
the forward reward
R_{f}
to change as episodes progress
in the form of

R^{k}_{f}=o_{k}\cdot k_{f}(x_{t+1}-x_{t})/\Delta t
where
o_{k}=a\text{sin}(wbk)
and
k
is a episode where
a,b>0
are constants.
A
positive
o_{k}
causes the agent to desire
a
forward
+x
direction as an optimal policy,
and a
negative
o_{k}
causes it to desire
a
backward
-x
direction. We
generate
different speeds of non-
stationarity
by changing the frequency variable
w\in\{1,2,3,4,5\}
.

Non-stationary variable
o_{k}
generator.

1.

Sine function: The non-stationary
parameter

o_{k}
is designed as

o_{k}=\sin{\left(2\pi wk/37\right)}
, where

w
is the integer speed of the environment change and
k
is the
episode number
. We
change

w

in the set

[1,2,3,4,5]
. We
divide

2\pi wk
by 37,
a
prime number, to
ensure
that the environment has various non-stationary modes and to avoid certain non-stationary
parameters
appearing frequently.

2.

Real data: we
bring
the stock price data to model
a
non-stationary real
dataset
.

Figure 4
:
Nonstationary parameter from real data A,B

Non-stationary
parameter

o_{k}
generator (ablation study).

B(G)
satisfies the property of the time-elapsing variation budget that
B(G)
increases as
G
increases. For the ablation study, we generate
o_{k}=\sin{(2\pi\cdot G\cdot k}/37)
, where

G\in\{38,76,114,152,190\}
. We estimated
B(G)
as
\sum_{k=1}^{150}|o_{k+1}-o_{k}|
:

G=38

G=76

G=114

G=152

G=190

B(G)

15.98

31.85

47.49

62.79

77.64

E.2
Hyperparameters and implementation details

Training Details.

For the ARIMA model that serves as a forecatser
f
, we
use the
auto
\_
arima function of pmdarima python package to find the optimal
p,q,d
. To compare the results between
ProST-G
and MBPO, we
train
the MBPO and
ProST-G
with
the
initial learning rate
lr=0.0003
with the decaying parameter
0.999
. For
ProST-G
, We
add the
uniform noise
\eta\sim\text{Unif}([-b,b])

to the
non-stationary
parameter

o^{k}
to generate
the
noisy non-stationary parameter
\hat{o}_{k}=o_{k}+\eta
with different noise bounds
b\in\{0.01,0.03,0.05\}
.
We denote

Unif
([-b,b])

as continuous uniform distributions over the interval
[-b,b]
.

To compare the
results
between
ProST-G

and
ProOLS, ONPG, FTML, we
train
these three baselines with eight different initial learning
rates

lr\in\{0.001,0.003,0.005,0.007,0.01,0.03,0.05,0.07\}
.

Hyper parameters.

Letter

hyper parameters

Swimmer-v2

Half cheetah-v2

Hopper-v2

K

episodes

100

150

150

H

environment steps per episodes

100

G

policy updates per epochs

50

\widehat{H}

model rollout length

1
\rightarrow
15 over episodes 20
\rightarrow
150

N

iteration of policy update and policy evaluation

1

M

model rollout batch size (
D_{syn}
)

1e5

\tau

entropy regularization parameter

0.2

\gamma

reward discounting factor

0.99

Note that
\widehat{H}
increases linearly within a certain range as episode goes by. We denote
h_{min}\rightarrow h_{max}
over episodes
k_{min}\rightarrow k_{max}
as
\widehat{H}(k)=\min(\max(h_{min}+(k-k_{min})/(k_{max}-k_{min})\cdot(h_{max}-h_{min}),h_{min}),h_{max})
.

Appendix F
Algorithms

F.1
ProST
framework

Set
:
k_{f}=1

Init
: policy
\pi^{0}
, forecaster
f_{\phi^{0}_{f}}
, model estimator
g_{\phi^{0}_{g}}
, two dataset
\mathcal{D}_{env},\mathcal{D}_{syn}

1

for

episode
k

do

2

Execute the agent with
\pi^{k}
in a environment
\mathcal{M}_{k}
and add a trajectory to
\mathcal{D}_{env}
.

/*
MDP forecaster
g\circ f
*/

/*
(1) Observe and forecast: */

Observe a noisy non-stationary parameter
\hat{o}_{k}

Update
f_{\phi_{f}},g_{\phi_{g}}
using
\mathcal{D}_{env}
and
\hat{o}_{k-(w-1):k}
.

Use
f_{\phi^{k}_{f}},g_{\phi^{k}_{g}}
to predict the future
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{k+1},\widehat{\mathcal{R}}^{k+1}
and construct future MDP
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{k+1}

/*
Baseline
A
*/

/*
(2) Optimize: */

Roll out synthetic trajectories in
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{k+1}
and add them to
\mathcal{D}_{syn}

3

Use
\mathcal{D}_{syn}
to evaluate and update
\pi^{k}
to
\widehat{\pi}^{k+1}

end for

4

Algorithm 1

ProST
framework

F.2
ProST-T
algorithm

Set
:
k_{f}=1

Init
: policy
\pi^{k}
, forecaster
f_{\phi^{k}_{f}}
, tabular reward model
g^{R}_{k}
, tabular transition probability model
g^{P}_{k}
, forecasted state-action value
\widehat{Q}^{\boldsymbol{\cdot},k+1}
, empty dataset
\mathcal{D}_{env},\mathcal{D}_{syn}

Explore
w
episodes and add
(\tau^{-k},\hat{o}_{-k})
to
D_{env}
where
k\in[w]
before starts

for

episodes
k=1,..,K

do

1

Rollout a trajectory
\tau_{k}
using
\pi^{k}
and
\mathcal{D}_{env}=\mathcal{D}_{env}\cup\{\tau_{k}\}

Observe a noisy non-stationary parameter
\hat{o}_{k}

/*
MDP forecaster
g\circ f
: (1) update
f,g
*/

Update
f_{\phi_{f}}
:
\phi^{k}_{f}\leftarrow\argmin_{\phi}\mathcal{L}_{f}(\hat{o}_{k-(w-1):k};\phi)

Update
g^{P}_{k}(s^{\prime},s,a,o)

Update
g^{R}_{k}(s,a,o)

/*
MDP forecaster
g\circ f
: (2) predict
\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{k+1},\widehat{\mathcal{R}}^{k+1}
*/

Forecast
1
episode ahead non-stationary parameter:
\hat{o}_{k+1}=f_{\phi^{k}_{f}}(\hat{o}_{k-(w-1):k})

Forecast transition probability function:
\widehat{g}^{P}_{k+1}=g^{P}_{k}(\cdot,\hat{o}_{k+1})

Forecast reward function:
\widehat{g}^{R}_{k+1}=g^{R}_{k}(\cdot,\hat{o}_{k+1})

Reset
\mathcal{D}_{syn}
to empty.

/*
Baseline
A
: NPG with entropy regularization */

Set
\widehat{\pi}^{(0)}\leftarrow\pi^{k}

for

g=0,..,G-1

do

2

Evaluate
Q_{\tau}^{\widehat{\pi}^{(g)}}
using the rollouts from the future model
\widehat{g}^{P}_{k+1},\widehat{g}^{R}_{k+1}

Update
\widehat{\pi}
:
\widehat{\pi}^{(g+1)}\leftarrow 1/Z^{(t)}\cdot\left(\widehat{\pi}^{(g)}\right)^{1-\frac{\eta\tau}{1-\gamma}}\exp{\left((\eta\widehat{Q}_{\tau}^{\widehat{\pi}^{(g)}})/(1-\gamma)\right)}

where
Z^{(t)}=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\left(\widehat{\pi}^{(g)}\right)^{1-\frac{\eta\tau}{1-\gamma}}\exp{\left((\eta\widehat{Q}_{\tau}^{\widehat{\pi}^{(g)}})/(1-\gamma)\right)}

end for

3

Set
\pi^{k+1}\leftarrow\widehat{\pi}^{(G)}

end for

4

Algorithm 2

ProST-T

F.3
ProST-G
algorithm

(1) Forecaster
f
.

We adopt the ARIMA model to forecast
\hat{o}_{k+1}
from the noisy
observation

\widehat{o}_{k-(w-1):k}
.
The
ARIMA model is one of the most general
classes
of models for forecasting a time series
, which
can be made to be stationary by taking a difference among the data. For given time series data
X_{t}
, we define ARIMA
(p,d,q)
as given by
X_{t}-\alpha_{1}X_{t-1}-\dots-\alpha_{p}X_{t-p}=\epsilon_{t}+\theta_{1}\epsilon_{t-1}+\cdots+\theta_{q}\epsilon_{t-q}
, where

\alpha_{i}
’s
are the parameters of the autoregressive part of the model, the
\theta_{i}
’s
are the parameters of the moving average part
, and
\epsilon_{t}
’s
are the error terms that take
d
times difference between
X_{t}
s, which we assume to be independent and follow a normal distribution with
a
zero mean.

(2) Model predictor
g
.

We use a bootstrap ensemble of dynamic models
\{g^{1}_{\phi_{g}},g^{2}_{\phi_{g}},...,g^{M}_{\phi_{g}}\}
. Each ensemble model is a probabilistic neural network whose output is parameterized by the mean vector
\mu
and the diagonal vector of the standard deviation
\text{Diag}(\Sigma)
of a Gaussian distribution
, namely

g^{i}_{\phi_{g}}(s_{h+1},r_{h}|s_{h},a_{h},\widehat{o}_{k+1})=\mathcal{N}(\mu^{i}_{\phi_{g}}(s_{h},a_{h}),\Sigma^{i}_{\phi_{g}}(s_{h},a_{h}))
. To efficiently handle uncertainty due to the non-stationary environment, we
design
each neural network to be a probabilistic model to capture the aleatoric uncertainty,
i.e.
the noise of the output, and learn multiple models as bootstrap ensemble to handle the epistemic uncertainty,
i.e.
the uncertainty in the model parameters. Then we predict
s_{h+1}

and

r_{h}
from a model uniformly chosen from its ensemble randomly that admits different transitions along a single model rollout to be sampled from different dynamics modes.

(3) Baseline algorithm
A
.

We adopt soft-actor critic (SAC) as our policy optimization algorithm. SAC alternates the policy evaluation step and the policy optimization step. For a given policy
\widehat{\pi}
, it estimates the forecasted
\widehat{Q}^{\widehat{\pi},k+1}
value using
the
Bellman backup operator and optimizes the policy that minimizes the expected KL-divergence between
\pi
and the exponential of the difference
\widehat{Q}^{\widehat{\pi},k+1}-\widehat{V}^{\widehat{\pi},k+1}
:
\mathbb{E}_{s\sim D_{syn}}[D_{KL}(\widehat{\pi}||\exp{(\widehat{Q}^{\widehat{\pi},k+1}-\widehat{V}^{\widehat{\pi},k+1})}]
.

Set
:
k_{f}=1

Init
: policy
\pi^{k}
, forecaster
f_{\phi^{k}_{f}}
, model estimator
g_{\phi^{k}_{g}}
, two dataset
\mathcal{D}_{env},\mathcal{D}_{syn}

1

Explore
w
episodes and add
(\tau^{-k},\hat{o}_{-k})
to
D_{env}
where
k\in[w]
before starts

for

episodes
k=1,..,K

do

2

Execute the agent with
\pi^{k}
in a environment
\mathcal{M}_{k}
and add a trajectory to
\mathcal{D}_{env}
.

/*
MDP forecaster
g\circ f
: (1) update
f,g
*/

Observe a noisy non-stationary variable
\hat{o}_{k}

Optimize
f_{\phi^{k}_{f}}
on
\hat{o}_{k-(w-1):k}

Optimize
g_{\phi^{k}_{g}}
on
\mathcal{D}_{env}

/*
MDP forecaster
g\circ f
: (2) predict
f,g
*/

Forecast
\hat{o}_{k+1}=f_{\phi^{k}_{f}}(\hat{o}_{k-(w-1):k})

Forecast model :
\widehat{g}_{k+1}=g_{\phi^{k}_{g}}(\cdot,\hat{o}_{k+1})

Reset
\mathcal{D}_{syn}
to empty.

/*
Baseline
A
: SAC */

Set
\widehat{\pi}^{k+1}\leftarrow\pi^{k}

for

epochs
n=1,...,N

do

3

for

model rollouts
m=1,..,M

do

4

Sample
\hat{s}^{m}_{0}
uniformly from
D_{env}
.

Perform a
\widehat{H}
-step model rollout using
\hat{a}^{m}_{h}=\widehat{\pi}^{k+1}(\hat{s}^{m}_{h})
,
\hat{s}^{m}_{h+1}=\widehat{g}_{k+1}(\hat{s}^{m}_{h},\hat{a}^{m}_{h})

and add a rollout to
\mathcal{D}_{syn}
.

end for

5

6

for

updates
g=1,..,G

do

7

Evaluate and update forecasted policy
\widehat{\pi}^{k+1}
on
\mathcal{D}_{syn}

end for

8

end for

9

Set
\pi_{k+1}\leftarrow\widehat{\pi}^{k+1}

end for

10
